It is the lifeblood of our planet, covering approximately 71% of its surface and constituting up to 60% of the human body. This seemingly ordinary substance plays a pivotal role in every aspect of life, from sustaining ecosystems to driving global economies.\n\nThe importance of water cannot be overstated. It is a universal solvent, capable of dissolving more substances than any other liquid, making it indispensable for various biological and chemical processes. In the human body, water serves as a medium for transporting nutrients, eliminating waste, and regulating temperature. It lubricates joints, cushions organs, and maintains the structure of cells. A well-hydrated body functions optimally, promoting overall health and well-being.\n\nBeyond its biological significance, water is a critical resource for agriculture, industry, and energy production. Agriculture, which accounts for 70% of global water consumption, relies on water to irrigate crops and raise livestock. Industries, such as manufacturing, mining, and textiles, require vast quantities of water for cooling, processing, and cleaning. Moreover, water is used to generate electricity through hydropower, a renewable and clean energy source.\n\nDespite its abundance, water is a finite resource, and its availability is increasingly threatened by climate change, pollution, and overexploitation. According to the United Nations, nearly 2 billion people live in countries experiencing high water stress, and by 2050, the global demand for water is expected to increase by 55%. To address these challenges, it is crucial to adopt sustainable water management practices, such as improving irrigation efficiency, reducing water waste, and promoting water conservation.\n\nWater pollution poses another significant threat to this vital resource. Industrial waste, agricultural runoff, and untreated sewage contaminate water sources, endangering aquatic life and human health. To combat water pollution, stringent regulations, innovative technologies, and public awareness campaigns are necessary. For instance, implementing advanced wastewater treatment systems, promoting sustainable agriculture practices, and encouraging responsible consumer behavior can help protect water quality.\n\nAccess to clean and safe water is a fundamental human right, yet millions of people worldwide lack this basic necessity. According to the World Health Organization, nearly 1 in 3 people do not have access to safe drinking water, and over 2 billion people lack access to basic sanitation services. Improving water infrastructure, investing in water treatment facilities, and promoting hygiene education are essential steps towards ensuring universal access to clean water and sanitation.\n\nIn conclusion, water is the indispensable elixir of life, underpinning every aspect of our existence. 'max'），则设为'不超过'；若上下文强调精确规格（如'exact length', 'precise count'），则设为'精确等于'。若上下文不明，应在标准中明确写出'精确等于N'或'不超过N'，并保留注释说明选择依据，防止因术语歧义导致评分失效。", "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null,
